````markdown
# Pace Runner

A web 3D runner MVP that turns a GPS run into a race course. The road's bends come from the real GPS track; its colours and per-segment top speed come from your real pace. You race a ghost ("past you") that replays that pace.

```bash
pnpm install
pnpm dev      # http://localhost:5173
pnpm test     # route processing + game simulation unit tests
pnpm build
```

## Docs
- [`docs/SPEC.md`](docs/SPEC.md) — full project specification (rules, algorithms, constants, UI, tests, acceptance checklist)
- [`docs/ARGON_BRIEF.md`](docs/ARGON_BRIEF.md) — hackathon product & build specification for Google Antigravity / Gemini 4 Argon (Track 1)
- [`docs/DESIGN.md`](docs/DESIGN.md) — mandatory visual design spec and the inspiration sites
- [`docs/GEMINI_GUIDE.md`](docs/GEMINI_GUIDE.md) — how to drive the Antigravity agent through the build, with prompts
- [`docs/REBUILD.md`](docs/REBUILD.md) — internal backup: every file verbatim with SHA-256 checksums (generated by `pnpm rebuild-doc`; never give it to the build agent)

## Flow
Home → **My Routes** grid → course preview → character select → start (Home → **My Stats** for your summary and main runner)

Routes are the runner's reward: every finished run becomes a collectible card with its route art (coloured by pace), real time and pace, and badges for best game time, coins and energy gels.

## Controls & rules
- Steer with ← → / A D, or by touching the left/right half of the screen
- Blue arrow pad = turbo (2 s)
- Items you **carry** (3 slots) and use with `Space` / `1`–`3` or by tapping a slot:
  - **Energy gel** (one at every 5 km of the real run, never within 300 m of the finish): 6 s with no pace speed cap (1.15× top speed) and a lighter grass penalty
  - **Banana**: 4 s of grip — no grass slowdown and much less drift in curves
  - **Shine muscat** (one grape): a 1.5 s sprint at 1.6× top speed
  - A full bag leaves new pickups on the road
- Gold coins = collectibles, laid out in straight, diagonal and arc rows; best coin count is saved per course
- Off the road (grass) = slowdown + camera shake
- Fall too far behind the ghost = game over

## Structure
| Path | Role |
|---|---|
| `src/route/` | GPX parsing, projection, RDP simplification, pace + 4 pace buckets, course building (pure functions) |
| `src/game/sim.ts` | Deterministic game simulation (speed caps, turbo, gels, coins, grass, centrifugal drift, ghost). `prepareCourse` records the ghost from a simulated perfect run |
| `src/game/constants.ts` | All balance values (speeds, turbo, gel, ghost slack, game-over gap) |
| `src/world/` | Three.js scene: toon shading, wind-blown instanced grass, trees, road ribbon, coins, gels, characters |
| `src/ui/minimap.ts` | Route drawing coloured by pace (HUD minimap, preview, route cards) |
| `src/main.ts` | Screen flow, camera, HUD, results |

## Rules of the road
- Pace colours: the run's [min, max] pace split into 4 equal ranges — green (fast) → yellow → orange → red (slow)
- Stretches without timestamps use the average pace
- Speed cap multipliers per colour: 1.0 / 0.85 / 0.7 / 0.55
- Course length scales with distance (1400–3200 units), so a game lasts roughly 1–1.5 minutes
- Overlapping routes: out-and-back stretches become side-by-side lanes, sharp GPS U-turns are rounded, crossings stay crossings, and the road you're on always draws on top

## MVP limits
- Phone sync is stood in for by 4 bundled sample runs (3K–10K); real data comes in via GPX upload
- The 5 characters (Hoodie, Sprout, Dizzy, Bloom, Beanie) are built from primitives and vertex gradients (`src/world/character.ts`). The **main runner** is chosen in My Stats, saved in localStorage, shown on Home and preselected for races
- **My Stats**: running summary, pace mix, and course bests with medals (computed by `src/game/stats.ts`)
````

````markdown
# Pace Runner — Hackathon Product & Build Specification

**Track:** Track 1 — *Gemini 4 Argon in entertainment* (gaming)
**Build tool:** Google Antigravity with Gemini 4 Argon (`gemini-4-argon`)
**Language of all deliverables:** English

> **Clean-slate statement.** This document is the **only** input for the build. It describes *what* to build and *how to build it*. It contains no source code. Everything is designed, written, tested and committed by the team and Antigravity during the hackathon. Open-source libraries and starter templates are allowed by the rules.

---

## 1. Track fit

### 1.1 Why Track 1
Track 1's first suggested 1-day build is *"a playable 3D browser game or procedural world built in Three.js or WebGPU where Gemini 4 Argon generates the mechanics and level geometry, gemini-3.8-live runs unscripted voice characters that trigger in-game function calls, and lyria-3.5 shifts the soundtrack dynamically."* Pace Runner is exactly that, with one twist: **the level is generated from the player's own real-world run.**

| Track 1 capability | How Pace Runner uses it |
|---|---|
| 3D scenes, physics, procedural worlds | Argon turns a GPS run into a Three.js level: road geometry, pace zones, pickups, set dressing. It then **playtests** the level in a headless simulation and revises it until it passes. |
| Multimodal orchestration, Argon as game master / showrunner | Argon directs a live race: **gemini-3.8-live** voices "Past You", an unscripted rival who calls in-game functions. **lyria-3.5** scores the race adaptively. **gemini-nano-banana-2.1** draws consistent character turnaround sheets. |
| Agentic video understanding (stretch goal) | Argon watches the recorded race and cuts a highlight reel that matches music beats and voice lines. |

### 1.2 Why not the other tracks
- **Track 2 (hard tech):** there's no hardware or physical-systems engineering target.
- **Track 3 (social good):** running is health-adjacent, but the product is entertainment and gives no health guidance. Positioning it as health would also require medical-grade human-in-the-loop safeguards for no benefit.
- **Track 4 (developer tools):** the product is for players, not developers.

### 1.3 Rubric mapping
| Criterion | Evidence we will show |
|---|---|
| Working demo on real input | The team's own real runs (GPX exports and running-app screenshots) become playable levels live, with no console errors |
| Argon / Managed Agents with verification loops | Four visible loops: level playtest, character render-and-critique, import reconciliation, grounded recap. Each one appears in an on-screen agent trace |
| Problem & impact | Tracked runs are "dead data". Turning them into replayable, shareable games gives recreational runners a reason to come back. It can grow into a product with sync, friends' ghosts and club leagues |
| Originality / only-with-Argon | Levels generated from your own body's history; a live voiced rival with game-function tools; 1M-context reasoning over a full running history; image → 3D character with self-critique |
| Clarity | Two-minute video and README built around one sentence: *"Your run, replayed as a game."* |

---

## 2. Product definition

### 2.1 One-liner and users
- **One-liner:** *Pace Runner turns the run you just finished into a 3D race against past you, directed live by Gemini 4 Argon.*
- **Primary users:** recreational runners (5–15 km) who track runs on a phone or watch but rarely revisit the data.
- **Secondary users:** running crews who want something fun to share after a group run.

### 2.2 Core loop
1. **Import a run:** a GPX file, or a screenshot of any running app's workout screen.
2. **Argon builds the level** from the route and pace, then playtests it until it is fair and fun.
3. **Pick or forge a runner:** five built-in characters, or forge one from any image.
4. **Race:** the course follows your real route, and a ghost of past you replays your real pacing. "Past You" talks to you live and the music reacts to the race.
5. **Result:** time vs past you, coins, and a recap grounded in your whole history. The level joins your **My Routes** trophy grid.

### 2.3 Game rules (requirements, not implementation)
- **Course shape:**
  - The route's shape and bends come from the GPS track, after projection, smoothing and simplification.
  - Course length scales with the real distance so that a race lasts about **60–120 seconds**.
  - **Overlapping routes must stay readable:**
    - Out-and-back stretches (the same street run twice) become **side-by-side lanes**, each moving to the side it leaves toward so it never crosses back over.
    - GPS U-turns that reverse within a sample or two are **rounded** so the road never folds back on itself.
    - Real crossings stay crossings.
    - The stretch the runner is on always draws on top.
    - Pickups from the other pass never appear on your road.
- **Pace zones:**
  - Split the run's pace range (fastest → slowest) into **four equal bands**: fast, steady, slow, very slow.
  - Colour the road by band: mint green, yellow, orange, red. The mint must be clearly distinct from the grass.
  - Each band caps top speed, so slower real stretches play slower.
  - Points with no timestamps use the run's average pace.
- **Controls:**
  - The runner moves forward automatically and the player only steers left/right: keyboard arrows or A/D, or touching the left/right half of the screen.
  - Curves push the runner outward in proportion to speed² × curvature.
- **Hazards:** leaving the road onto grass slows you down hard, with a camera shake.
- **Pickups:**
  - Turbo pads give a short burst above the speed cap.
  - Coins come in rows (straight, diagonal or arc) and only count toward the score.
- **Carried items** (picked up into **3 slots**, used when the player chooses: `Space` uses the oldest, `1`–`3` a slot, or tap the slot):
  - **Energy gel:** one at every **5 km of the real run**, never within 300 m of the finish. Lifts the pace cap for about 6 s.
  - **Banana:** about 4 s of grip: no grass slowdown and much less curve drift.
  - **Shine muscat** (a single grape): a 1.5 s sprint well above the pace cap.
  - Bananas and muscats lie on the road every few hundred metres, away from pads and gels.
  - Carrying an item does nothing until it is used. A full bag leaves new pickups on the road and says so.
- **Ghost ("Past You"):**
  - The ghost's timing comes from a simulated clean run of the level, made a few percent slower.
  - A clean line beats it narrowly, turbos win time, and sloppy driving loses.
  - Falling more than a set gap behind is **game over**.
- **Start and scoring:**
  - A 3-2-1-GO countdown.
  - Best time and best coin count are saved per level.
  - The result screen shows the time difference to past you and an "equivalent real pace".
- **Feel and style:** **follow [`DESIGN.md`](./DESIGN.md) exactly.** It is mandatory and part of every Gate. It covers colour tokens, typography, components, every screen layout (desktop and mobile), the 3D art direction, the five characters part by part, pickups, the route-map style, motion and sound, plus the inspiration sites.
  - In short: a pastel, toy-like, toon-shaded meadow at golden hour, with wind-blown grass, round trees and soft fog.
  - All UI copy is in English.
  - Titles use **Hanalei Fill** and body text uses **Lexend** (Google Fonts).

### 2.4 Screens
**Home → My Routes grid → Level preview → Character select → Race → Result / Game over**, plus **My Stats** (from Home and My Routes) and an always-available **Agent trace** drawer.

- **My Routes:** a grid of reward cards. Each card shows the route art coloured by pace, the distance, the real time and pace, and badges (new / best time, coins, gels). The last card is "+ Add a run".
- **Level preview:** the route map, stats, the pace legend with real pace ranges, and Argon's level notes ("Coach picks").
- **Character select:** five built-in runners plus **+ Forge from image**. The player's **main runner** is preselected; picking another changes only this race.
- **My Stats:**
  - a **main-runner** picker (saved; shown on Home and preselected for races);
  - a **running summary**: total distance, total time, runs, average pace, longest run, fastest run;
  - a **pace mix**: the share of all distance in each pace zone;
  - **course bests**: best time, best coins and a medal per route (gold: beat past you by ≥ 5 %, silver: beat past you, bronze: finished).
- **Race HUD:**
  - time and coins;
  - the gap to past you;
  - a minimap of the whole route with your position and the ghost's;
  - the current pace zone and its real pace;
  - **3 item slots** and an active-effect bar (energy / grip / sprint);
  - live "Past You" subtitles.

---

## 3. Gemini features

Each feature is an agent with a verification loop. The **Managed Agents** orchestrator runs them, and every step is streamed to the Agent trace drawer: input summary, output summary, verdict, iteration count and duration.

### A1 — Level Director (Argon) ★ the Track 1 centrepiece
- **Input:** run data (route, per-point pace, splits) plus a theme request (e.g. "Seoul riverside at sunset").
- **Output (`LevelSpec` JSON):**
  - road segments with pace zones;
  - turbo pad and coin-row placements, with a "reason" for each;
  - gel stations;
  - set-dressing themes per section (trees, flowers, river, city props);
  - sky and lighting mood;
  - music cue points for A4;
  - short "Coach picks" notes.
- **Verification loop — headless playtest** (at most 4 iterations):
  1. **Schema and bounds:** the spec validates, every object is on or beside the road, and nothing overlaps.
  2. **Bot playtests:** run 3 bots through the deterministic simulation: a perfect line, an average player and a sloppy player.
  3. **Fairness targets:** the perfect bot beats the ghost; the sloppy bot loses; race duration is within 60–120 s; at least one turbo opportunity per 400 m; no unwinnable stretch.
  4. On failure, Argon receives the measured numbers and revises the spec.
- **Fallback:** a deterministic procedural layout, labelled "procedural (no AI)".

### A2 — Character Forge (Argon + gemini-nano-banana-2.1)
1. The user uploads any image they own: a mascot, pet or selfie.
2. **nano-banana-2.1** produces a consistent **turnaround sheet** (front, side, back) in the toy art style.
3. **Argon** writes a `CharacterSpec` JSON made of primitive parts and colours on a fixed rig. The client never runs model-written code.
4. **Verification loop** (at most 5 iterations; passes when the critique score is ≥ 85 and every deterministic check passes):
   - **Validate:** the spec matches the schema (≤ 40 parts, bounded numbers, `#rrggbb` colours).
   - **Render:** front, ¾ and side views plus a run-pose frame in a headless browser.
   - **Deterministic checks:**
     - height within the allowed range;
     - feet on the ground and nothing below it;
     - **every face part is in front of the head surface**, checked by raycast;
     - left/right symmetry;
     - the thumbnail is not cropped.
   - **Critique:** Argon compares the renders with the turnaround sheet and returns `{score, pass, issues:[{part, problem, fix}]}`.
   - **Patch:** Argon revises the spec. The diff is logged.
5. **Human in the loop:** the user must **Approve** before the character appears in the picker.
6. The five built-in characters are themselves authored as `CharacterSpec`s during the build. That proves the format works and gives Argon few-shot examples.

### A3 — "Past You" live voice rival (gemini-3.8-live)
- **What it is:** a voiced, unscripted rival character speaking in the player's own competitive voice persona. It reacts to the race in real time.
- **Live state it receives:** gap to the ghost, pace zone, pickups, off-road events, distance left.
- **Tools it may call (function calling):**
  - `taunt(text)` — subtitle plus voice
  - `cheer(text)`
  - `requestCoinTrail(pattern)` — spawns a coin row at least 80 m ahead
  - `requestMusicIntensity(level)`
  - `announceSplit(km)`
- **Verification and guardrails:** a rules engine validates every tool call before it runs:
  - argument ranges and placement legality (it may never place objects behind the player or on top of hazards);
  - rate limits (at most one spawn per 15 s);
  - a content filter (friendly competitive tone; no health or medical claims).
  - Rejected calls are returned to the model with the reason.
- **Controls:** a mute toggle, and subtitles always on.

### A4 — Adaptive soundtrack (lyria-3.5)
- **Generation:** Argon, as showrunner, writes a short music brief per level, based on its theme and the section cue points from A1. Lyria generates layered stems.
- **Mixing:** the client crossfades layers by game state:
  - pace zone sets energy;
  - a growing gap to the ghost sets tension;
  - turbo and gel trigger a lift;
  - the finish plays an outro.
- **Verification:** generated audio must exist, decode, loop cleanly and match the requested duration. Otherwise retry once, then use a silent fallback with synthesized UI sounds.

### A5 — Run-from-anything import (Argon, multimodal)
- **Input:** GPX (parsed locally, no model call) **or** a screenshot of a running app's workout screen showing the map, splits and summary.
- **Extraction:** Argon returns the summary (distance, duration, average pace, date), per-km splits, and a normalised route polyline traced from the map, each with a confidence value.
- **Verification — numeric reconciliation** (at most 3 retries, with the exact mismatch fed back):
  - the split times sum to the duration within ±3 %;
  - the number of splits matches the distance;
  - the average pace equals duration ÷ distance within ±3 %;
  - the route is continuous, with no segment longer than 15 % of the total.
- **Human in the loop:** an overlay of the traced route on the screenshot plus the extracted numbers. The user picks **Looks right** or edits a value.

### A6 — Long-context recap and rival pick (Argon, 1M context)
- **Input:** the player's whole history in one request: every run, every point and split.
- **Output:** a recap of at most 120 words, a headline, and the **best rival run** to race next, with a reason.
- **Tools:** deterministic stats functions (per-run stats, run comparison, pace by km).
- **Verification:** every number in the text must equal a tool result, and the rival must exist. Unsupported claims are sent back for regeneration (at most 3 tries).

### A7 — Highlight reel (stretch goal)
- **Generation:** record the race canvas. Argon (agentic video understanding) picks the best 15 s around the overtakes, gel pickups and finish, aligned to music beats and "Past You" lines.
- **Optional:** gemini-omni-1.1-flash renders a stylised intro card.
- **Verification:** every cut timestamp lies within the recording, and every event it claims appears in the event log.

> **Model access.** Model names follow the hackathon's track description: `gemini-4-argon`, `gemini-3.8-live`, `gemini-nano-banana-2.1`, `lyria-3.5`, `gemini-omni-1.1-flash`, and the `antigravity-preview-09-2026` sandbox. Before writing integration code, confirm each model's availability, SDK calls, parameters (e.g. `thinking_level="high"` for Level Director planning) and quotas in the official Google AI Studio / Agent Platform documentation. Do not guess API shapes.

---

## 4. Architecture

```
Browser — Vite + TypeScript + three.js + gsap   (Firebase Hosting)
  ├─ game: deterministic simulation, renderer, HUD, input, audio mixer
  ├─ live voice session (gemini-3.8-live, via short-lived token from the server)
  └─ /api/** (fetch + Server-Sent Events for live agent progress)
        │
Cloud Run "pace-runner-api" — Node + TypeScript
  ├─ Managed Agents orchestrator: LevelDirector · CharacterForge · Importer · Recap · Showrunner(music)
  ├─ Gemini 4 Argon + companion models via the official SDK (service account; no keys in the browser)
  ├─ Verifiers: JSON-schema validation, headless playtest (shared simulation module),
  │            headless renderer (Chromium + three.js) for Forge views, numeric reconcilers
  ├─ Tool-call rules engine for the live rival
  └─ Storage: Firestore (runs, levels, characters, traces) + Cloud Storage (images, audio, renders)
```

- **One shared simulation module** is used by both the browser and the server playtests, so the bots experience exactly what players do.
- **Offline fallback:** GPX import, procedural levels and built-in characters work with **zero** API calls.
- **Security and cost:**
  - per-IP rate limits;
  - upload size limits;
  - images and audio stored privately;
  - a daily budget guard that switches AI features to "cached demo" mode, clearly labelled.

### 4.1 Data contracts (JSON Schema files in `/shared/schemas`)
- **`RunData`:** id, name, date, points[{lat, lon, t?}], splits[{km, paceSec}], source ("gpx" | "screenshot").
- **`LevelSpec`:**
  - id, runId, theme;
  - segments[{fromM, toM, zone}];
  - pads[{s, lateral, reason}];
  - coinRows[{s, pattern, lateral, count}];
  - gels[{s, km}];
  - dressing[{fromM, toM, set}];
  - sky, lighting;
  - musicCues[{s, mood}];
  - notes[].
- **`CharacterSpec`:**
  - name, blurb, scale, hipY, armRest;
  - body.parts[], head{y, parts[], face};
  - arm{pivot, length, radius, paint, tip[]}, leg{pivot, length, radius, paint, tip[]}.
  - A **part** has: shape ∈ {sphere, capsule, torus, cylinder, box, halfSphere, lathe, tube}, params, position, rotation, scale, and paint (a hex colour, or a bottom→top gradient of ≤ 4 colours).
  - A **face** has: eyes ∈ {googly, happy, beads, x, dots}, mouth ∈ {smile, lips, open}, plus optional cheeks and nose.
  - At most 40 parts; every number bounded.
- **`ToolCall`:** name, args, issuedAt, verdict ("accepted" | "rejected"), reason.
- **`Trace`:** agent, step, iteration, inputSummary, outputSummary, verdict, ms, cached:boolean.

---

## 5. Build specification

### 5.1 Environment
| Item | Choice |
|---|---|
| Agentic IDE | Google Antigravity, agent model `gemini-4-argon`; sandbox `antigravity-preview-09-2026` for test harnesses |
| Prompt prototyping | Google AI Studio (system prompts for each agent, saved to `/prompts/*.md` in the repo) |
| Runtime | Node 22+, pnpm |
| Client | Vite, TypeScript (strict), three.js, gsap |
| Server | Cloud Run (Node/TS), Firestore, Cloud Storage |
| Hosting | Firebase Hosting with `/api/**` rewritten to Cloud Run (region `asia-northeast3`) |
| Tests | Vitest (unit and simulation), Playwright (end-to-end and visual checks in Antigravity's browser) |
| CI | GitHub Actions: typecheck, unit tests, schema tests, build on every push |

### 5.2 Repository layout (new public repo, created at kickoff)
```
pace-runner/
  apps/web/          game client (scenes, sim adapter, HUD, screens, audio mixer, live-voice client)
  apps/api/          Cloud Run service (agents, verifiers, rules engine, renderer, SSE)
  packages/sim/      deterministic course builder + simulation + bots (shared by web and api)
  packages/schemas/  JSON Schemas + generated TypeScript types
  prompts/           system prompts per agent (versioned)
  fixtures/          the team's own sample runs (GPX + screenshots) and test images the team owns
  docs/              BUILD_LOG.md (timestamped), ARCHITECTURE.md, DEMO.md
  .github/workflows/ci.yml
  LICENSE (MIT) · README.md
```

### 5.3 How each phase is executed in Antigravity
Every phase follows the same agent loop:
1. **Plan:** Argon writes a short plan into `docs/BUILD_LOG.md` (time, goal, files).
2. **Implement** in small commits.
3. **Verify:** typecheck and unit tests in the sandbox, then a Playwright run in Antigravity's browser with screenshots.
4. **Compare** the results with the phase's **Gate** list below. If anything fails, fix and repeat.
5. **Record:** add the evidence to the build log (test output summary, screenshot paths) and commit as `phase N: <what>`.

**A phase is never marked done without its Gate evidence.**

### 5.4 Phases and time boxes (one-day sprint, ~14 working hours)
| Phase | Time | Work | Gate (must pass) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P0 Setup** | H0–H1 | Public repo, monorepo skeleton, CI, Firebase + Cloud Run hello-world, schema package | CI green; hosted page loads; `/api/health` returns 200 |
| **P1 Core game** | H1–H4 | GPX import, course builder (incl. lanes for overlapping passes and U-turn rounding), pace zones, simulation, ghost, pickups, carried items, controls, HUD, toon meadow, screens, My Stats + main runner, fonts | Unit tests for pace bands, gel placement, simulation outcomes (clean beats ghost, grass → game over, turbo faster), items (inventory cap, each effect), stats, and an out-and-back + U-turn + figure-8 GPX fixture (lanes separate, crossings kept, never folds back, playable to the finish); a team member's real GPX plays end to end on desktop and phone; every item in the DESIGN.md §8 checklist passes |
| **P2 Level Director (A1)** | H4–H6.5 | `LevelSpec`, Argon prompt, bot playtest verifier, trace streaming, procedural fallback | 3 real runs produce levels that pass the playtest within 4 iterations; the trace shows each iteration |
| **P3 Character Forge (A2)** | H6.5–H9 | `CharacterSpec` interpreter, 5 built-in specs, nano-banana turnaround, headless renderer, checks + critique loop, Approve UI | 3 owned images forge approved characters within 5 iterations; the face-in-front check catches a seeded bad spec |
| **P4 Live rival + music (A3, A4)** | H9–H11 | Live session, tool schema, rules engine, subtitles, mute; Lyria stems + adaptive mixer | During a race, ≥ 3 accepted tool calls change the game, and an illegal call is visibly rejected; music layers respond to zone, gap and turbo |
| **P5 Import + recap (A5, A6)** | H11–H12.5 | Screenshot extraction + reconciliation + confirm overlay; long-context recap with grounded numbers | Screenshots from 2 different apps import within ±3 %; 0 unsupported numbers across 5 recaps |
| **P6 Deploy + demo data** | H12.5–H13.5 | Production deploy, budget guard, cached-demo labels, seed the team's real runs | Public URL: no console errors on Chrome/Safari/iOS/Android; the game still works with the API blocked |
| **P7 Submission** | H13.5–H14 | README, Project Description, 2-minute video | Checklist in §6.4 complete |

**Stretch goal if time remains:** A7 highlight reel.

**Cut order if behind:** A7 → A6 → music falls back to synthesized layers → screenshot import falls back to GPX only. **Never cut** A1, A2 or the trace drawer: they carry the verification-loop score.

### 5.5 Test harness details
- **Simulation bots** (in `packages/sim`), all deterministic with a fixed timestep:
  - **perfect:** cancels curve drift exactly and stays centred;
  - **average:** steers with reaction noise and grabs about 50 % of pads;
  - **sloppy:** misses pads and drifts onto the grass periodically.
- **Fixtures:** real GPX runs (3–10 km), a run with missing timestamps, a run whose timestamps go backwards, a very short run, an **out-and-back run with a sharp U-turn and a figure-8**, and app screenshots.
- **Visual checks:** Playwright screenshots of every screen at 1280×800 and 390×844, attached to the build log.
- **Contract tests:**
  - every agent output must validate against its schema;
  - each verifier has a "known bad" fixture it must reject.

### 5.6 Content and licensing
- Use only images, audio and characters the team **owns or has licensed**. Do not ship watermarked stock images in the public repo or the video.
- Credit the fonts (Hanalei Fill, Lexend: SIL OFL) and libraries in the README.
- Don't use third-party brand logos (running apps, gel makers) in game assets.

## 6. Submission package

### 6.1 Project Description (draft)
> **Pace Runner — your run, replayed as a game.**
> Recreational runners track every run, but the data dies the moment they stop their watch. Pace Runner turns any run, whether a GPX file or just a screenshot from your running app, into a 3D race on your real route against a ghost of *past you*.
>
> **Gemini 4 Argon is the game master:**
> - it **builds the level** from your route and pace, then playtests it with simulated bots until it's fair;
> - it **forges your runner** from any image (with gemini-nano-banana-2.1 turnarounds), critiquing its own 3D renders until they match;
> - it directs **"Past You"**, a live gemini-3.8-live rival who talks trash and calls in-game functions through a verified rules engine;
> - it scores the race with adaptive **lyria-3.5** stems;
> - it reads your entire history in one 1M-token context to pick your next rival.
>
> Every AI step runs in a verification loop you can watch in the agent trace, and you approve every character and imported run.
>
> **Built during the hackathon with Google Antigravity + Gemini 4 Argon.** Track 1: Entertainment.

### 6.2 README outline
1. Pitch + GIF
2. Demo link + video
3. How to play
4. How Gemini 4 Argon is used: each agent, its verification loop and its fallback
5. Architecture diagram
6. What's live / cached / simulated
7. Setup (local + deploy)
8. Build log summary
9. Credits and license

### 6.3 Demo video (~2:00)
| Time | Shot |
|---|---|
| 0:00–0:10 | A runner stops their watch: "Your run is dead data." |
| 0:10–0:30 | Drop a running-app screenshot → reconciliation ✓ → Looks right |
| 0:30–0:50 | Level Director: the trace shows playtest iterations (perfect / average / sloppy bots) until PASS |
| 0:50–1:10 | Character Forge: turnaround sheet → renders → score climbing → Approve |
| 1:10–1:40 | The race: pace-coloured real route, the ghost, "Past You" speaking live and spawning a coin trail, the music lifting on the gel |
| 1:40–1:52 | Result + grounded recap + "Race your rival" + the My Routes trophy grid |
| 1:52–2:00 | "Built with Gemini 4 Argon in Antigravity during the hackathon." Track 1. |

### 6.4 Final checklist
- [ ] All demo code written during the event; the public repo's history shows steady progress and `docs/BUILD_LOG.md` is timestamped.
- [ ] Real inputs (the team's own runs) work end to end on the public URL with no console errors.
- [ ] A1–A6 loops are visible in the trace; anything cached is labelled; fallbacks work with the API blocked.
- [ ] Human-in-the-loop: character approval, import confirmation, and a mute/subtitle option for the live voice.
- [ ] Only owned or licensed assets; credits and an MIT license included.
- [ ] Project Description, README and video in English; Track 1 stated.

## 7. Future development (NOT in the hackathon scope)

> Everything in this section is **out of scope for the hackathon build**. Do not schedule it in §5.4, do not add it to the Gates, and do not show it as a live feature in the description or video. It may be mentioned once as "What's next".

### 7.1 X1 — World Director: GPX → the world of *that* run
**Idea:** from a single GPX file, Argon recreates the run's place, time, weather and mood as the game world, and directs the music to match. The same route run at dawn in spring and at night in the rain becomes two different worlds.

**Inputs derived from the GPX:**
- **Environment:** per-section surroundings (riverside, bridge, city blocks, park, hill) and nearby landmarks, from Google Maps grounding along the route.
- **Time:** local time of day and season, from the timestamps and the route's time zone.
- **Weather:** the actual conditions at that time and place (clear, rain, fog, temperature), from a historical weather source.
- **Mood:** the race's emotional arc, from the pace profile (easy start → struggle on the hill → final kick).

**Output (`WorldSpec` JSON), applied on top of the base level:**
- biome sections chosen from a **fixed, pre-built prop library** of 8–10 biomes (Argon picks combinations, placement, colour and mood; it never invents geometry);
- landmarks shown as simplified toy props;
- sky, lighting, fog and weather effects;
- palette and mood keywords;
- music cues per section, handed to lyria-3.5.

Every field also records where its value came from: `source: "data" | "default" | "user"`.

**Verification loop:**
1. **Grounded places:** every place or landmark in the spec must appear in the map grounding results; invented places are rejected and regenerated.
2. **Time and weather consistency:** the sky and lighting must match the local time of day at the timestamps and the recorded weather (e.g. a 21:00 run must not have a midday sky).
3. **Route alignment:** biome boundaries must line up with real route positions (e.g. the bridge section actually crosses the water).
4. **Visual critique:** headless screenshots of each section are compared by Argon with the spec's intent; Argon scores and patches.
5. **Fairness:** the base level's bot playtest still passes. A pretty world that plays unfairly fails.

**Safety and control:**
- **Privacy zones, on by default:** start and finish points are usually near home. Hide or blur a radius around both on every shared surface (cards, minimap, screenshots, video).
- **Human in the loop:** the user sees a world preview and picks **Build this world** or **Regenerate**, and can override any value.

### 7.2 Default settings when information is missing
When an input is missing, invalid or its lookup fails, X1 falls back to the defaults below **without asking**.
- The world preview marks each defaulted value with a **"Default"** badge so the user can see and change it.
- The `WorldSpec` records `source: "default"` for that field.
- A run with *no* usable information at all gets exactly the base game's standard look: the pastel meadow at golden hour.

| Input | Missing when | Default |
|---|---|---|
| Environment / biome | no map grounding results, or the lookup fails | **Pastel meadow** for the whole route: grass, round trees, flowers |
| Landmarks | none found, or not verified by grounding | **None.** No landmark props are placed |
| Time of day | the GPX has no timestamps | **Golden hour:** late afternoon, low warm sun, peach horizon |
| Time zone | it can't be derived from coordinates | The **device's time zone** |
| Season | the GPX has no date | **Late spring:** fresh greens, a few blossoms |
| Weather | no timestamp or location, or the weather lookup fails | **Clear and mild:** about 18 °C, light breeze, no rain or fog |
| Mood arc | no pace data (only the route) | **Steady build:** calm start, rising energy at about 70 % of the course, big finish |
| Pace zones | no timestamps at all | Every point uses the run's **average pace**, or a 6:00 /km assumption if no average is known, so the whole road is one zone |
| Music brief | no mood or theme, or the brief generation fails | **Upbeat lo-fi pop, ~105 BPM**, four layered stems (base, drums, lead, finale) |
| Music audio | lyria-3.5 unavailable, or the output fails verification | **Synthesized layers** in the browser, with the same adaptive mixing rules |
| Run name | the GPX has no `<name>` | `"Run on <date>"`, or `"My run"` if there's no date |
| Run date | no timestamps | The **import date** |
| Privacy zone | (always applied unless the user turns it off) | **On, 200 m** around the start and finish |

### 7.3 Other later ideas
- Automatic sync from running platforms (subject to their API terms; check AI-use restrictions before sending their data to any model).
- Friends' ghosts and crew leagues.
- A native phone companion that reads workout routes directly from the phone's health store.
````

### `docs/DESIGN.md`

24318 bytes · sha256 `b6d9fc18354bfdb82b04e87d5692382feb4411b95037226282ce27791a9e5499`

```markdown
# Pace Runner — Design Specification

The visual and interaction design for Pace Runner. It is **mandatory**: the build must match it. It describes *what things look like*, never how to code them. Every colour is a hex value; every size is in CSS px (UI) or world units (3D, where the runner is about 1.8 units tall and the road is 9 units wide).

**Mood in one line:** a pastel, toy-like summer meadow at golden hour. Soft, rounded, friendly, never neon, never dark.

---

## 1. Design tokens

### 1.1 Colour
| Token | Hex | Use |
|---|---|---|
| ink | `#2b2233` | primary text, outlines, dark stripes |
| ink-soft | `#6b6275` | secondary text, captions |
| paper | `#fffaf2` | panels |
| paper-2 | `#fff3e3` | stat tiles, secondary buttons, chips on panels |
| card | `#fffaf4` | reward cards, stat cards |
| coral | `#ff8a7a` | primary button, logo, player dot, accents |
| coral-dark | `#f06a5a` | primary button lip shadow |
| coral-deep | `#f2677d` | start/finish dots, pin gradient end |
| pace-fast | `#3fc39a` | mint green; must read clearly against the grass |
| pace-steady | `#f5d04f` | yellow |
| pace-slow | `#f5a04f` | orange |
| pace-very-slow | `#ee6767` | red |
| lilac-shadow | `#8e72b8` | soft coloured shadows (used at 14–35 % opacity) |
| gel-red | `#e8414f` | energy gel, gel badges, energy bar |
| turbo-cyan | `#5fd3ff` | turbo pads, turbo screen flash |

Overlay backdrop for full-screen pages (My Routes, My Stats): a vertical gradient from `rgba(232,222,248,0.72)` (lilac, top) to `rgba(255,236,226,0.78)` (peach, bottom), with a 14 px backdrop blur over the 3D scene.

### 1.2 Typography (Google Fonts)
| Role | Font | Weight | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| Logo / screen titles / panel titles | **Hanalei Fill** (single weight, never fake-bolded) | 400 | logo: `min(13vw, 22vh)` desktop, `min(22vw, 16vh)` mobile, line-height 0.92; page titles: `clamp(48px, 8vw, 84px)`; panel titles: 34 px |
| Everything else | **Lexend** | 400–800 | body 15–18 px; buttons 17–20 px, 600; stat values 18 px, 600; captions 12–13 px |
| Eyebrow labels | Lexend | 600 | 13 px, letter-spacing 0.18em, ink-soft |
| Numbers (timer, stats) | Lexend | 600–700 | tabular figures |

All copy is **English**. No text effects on titles (no outlines, gradients or 3D).

### 1.3 Shape and depth
- **Radii:** panels 28 px; cards 26 px; stat tiles 14 px; badges and pills fully round (999 px); item slots 18 px.
- **"Lip" shadow (toy feel):** solid, no blur, offset straight down. Buttons 5 px; chips 4 px; cards 6 px. The colour is `rgba(43,34,51,0.07–0.15)`, or coral-dark for primary buttons.
- **Soft lift:** cards add `0 18px 34px rgba(142,114,184,0.14)`; panels add `0 20px 40px rgba(43,34,51,0.12)`.
- **Press:** buttons move 3 px down and the lip shrinks to 2 px. **Hover:** lift by 2–4 px.

### 1.4 Components
| Component | Look |
|---|---|
| Primary button | coral fill, white Lexend 600, pill, 5 px coral-dark lip |
| Secondary button | paper-2 fill, ink text, pill, 5 px neutral lip |
| Back button | 40 px circle, paper-2 (or 80 % white on overlays), "←" |
| Chip (HUD) | `rgba(255,250,242,0.92)` pill, 4 px lip, Lexend 600 |
| Badge | small pill, 12 px, 700: **New** lilac `#e6dcff` / `#5b3fa0`; **★ best** butter `#ffe9a8` / `#8a5a00`; **coins** paper-2 with an orange "$"; **gels** pink `#ffdbe0` / `#b8283a` |
| Stat tile | paper-2 rounded tile: 12 px ink-soft label above an 18 px value |
| Card | card colour, 26 px radius, lip + soft lift; on hover lifts 4 px and tilts −0.4° |

---

## 2. Screens

Layouts are given for **desktop (≥ 721 px)** and **mobile (≤ 720 px)**. The 3D scene always fills the background.

### 2.1 Home
- **Desktop:** text block on the left, about 52 % of the width, padded 7 vw from the left edge, over a left-to-right paper fade (85 % → transparent at 70 % of the width). From top to bottom:
  - the logo "PACE / RUNNER" on two lines in coral;
  - the tagline "The route you ran becomes the course." (Lexend, ~20–26 px, ink-soft);
  - a full-width (max 480 px) primary button **My Runs**;
  - under it, a secondary button **My Stats**.

  The **main runner** stands on the right of the screen, idling on the road, with the camera slowly swaying around its front.
- **Mobile:** the character is in the upper half. The logo, tagline and both buttons sit at the bottom over a bottom-to-top paper fade.
- No eyebrow line and no extra marketing copy.

### 2.2 My Routes (the runner's rewards — the centrepiece)
- **Overlay page** with the lilac→peach backdrop. Centred header:
  - a back button at top-left and a secondary **My Stats** button at top-right;
  - the eyebrow "Every run you finish becomes a course";
  - the title "My Routes" in Hanalei Fill;
  - the summary line "`N` routes · `X` km · `M` raced".
- **Grid:** auto-fill columns at least 250 px wide, max width 1120 px, 18 px gaps. On mobile, exactly 2 columns with 12 px gaps.
- **Route card:**
  - **Art area** (4:3): a soft two-colour pastel gradient. The tint cycles per card: pink→blue, blue→butter, butter→pink, mint→lilac. A white radial highlight sits at the top-left. The route is drawn on it in the map style (§5). A teardrop pin sits at top-left, and a white pill distance tag (e.g. "9.9 km", 13 px 700) at top-right.
  - **Info area:**
    - the run name (Lexend 18 px 700);
    - the date (13 px, ink-soft);
    - a row "⏱ 54:12   ↗ 5'28"/km" (15 px, 600, tabular);
    - a badge row.
- **Last card:** "+ Add a run" as a dashed lilac border (3 px, 45 % opacity) with no fill. It has a 54 px white circle "+", "Add a run" (lilac `#6b5a8e`, 700) and "Upload a GPX file". Tapping it opens the file picker.
- Cards stagger in from 18 px below, 50 ms apart.

### 2.3 Course preview
- **Desktop:** a single panel (max 440 px) docked on the right, about 5 vw from the edge, vertically centred. Behind it, the 3D camera slowly orbits high above the whole course.
- **Mobile:** the panel docks to the bottom at full width.
- **Panel contents, top to bottom:**
  - the date (eyebrow) and the run name (Hanalei Fill 34 px);
  - the route map (§5) at aspect ~1.35, with a 20 px radius;
  - a 3-tile stat row: Distance / Real time / Avg pace;
  - the pace legend: 2×2 rows of a colour pill, the label (Fast / Steady / Slow / Very slow) and the real pace range;
  - the how-to hint (14 px, ink-soft);
  - the primary button **Run this course**.

### 2.4 Character select
- **Panel:** max 660 px, docked like the preview.
  - The eyebrow is the course name, the title is "Who's running?".
  - **5 character cards** sit in one row on desktop and in a 3 + 2 grid on mobile.
  - The primary button **Start running** is at the bottom.
- **Character card:**
  - **Thumbnail:** a square with a 14 px radius on a sky→peach gradient, `#d9effa` → `#fff3e3`, showing the 3D character in a ¾ idle pose.
  - **Text:** the name (Lexend 600) and a one-line blurb (12 px, ink-soft).
  - **States:**
    - **Selected:** a 3 px coral border and a white background.
    - **Main runner:** a small coral "Main" pill under the blurb.
- **Camera:** on wide screens the camera pans so the 3D character stands to the **left** of the panel. Picking a card swaps the character with an elastic squash-pop (it starts 0.6 wide × 1.3 tall and springs to 1:1 in 0.6 s).

### 2.5 My Stats
Overlay page like My Routes, with the title "My Stats" and the eyebrow "Your running at a glance". Cards sit in a 2-column grid (1 column on mobile):
1. **Main runner** (full width): the same 5 character cards; the chosen one has a coral border and a "Main" pill.
2. **Running summary:** a 2×3 grid of stat tiles: Total distance, Total time, Runs, Avg pace, Longest run (with the run name as an 11 px caption), Fastest run (with its name).
3. **Pace mix:** a 22 px tall rounded stacked bar in the four pace colours, sized by share of distance, plus a 2×2 legend with percentages.
4. **Course bests** (full width): one white row per course containing:
   - a medal (🥇 / 🥈 / 🥉, or a grey "·");
   - the name with km underneath;
   - the best time in bold tabular figures, or "Not raced";
   - a paper-2 pill "$ best/total".

### 2.6 Race HUD
- **Top-left stack:**
  - a time chip (30 px, tabular) and a coin chip with a coin icon and "`n` / total" (the total in 14 px ink-soft);
  - under them, an **effect bar** while an item is active: a pill with a small icon, the label and a white progress track. It is gel-red for "Energy boost", banana-yellow `#e0a81e` for "Grip" and muscat-green `#6fae2a` for "Sprint", each with a darker 4 px lip;
  - the **gap box**: a 230 px card with a 13 px label ("Side by side with past you" / "48m ahead of past you" / "12m behind past you") and a 10 px track with a centre tick. It fills right in mint when ahead, and left in orange, turning red as the gap nears game over.
- **Top-right:**
  - the minimap (190 × 160 desktop, 120 × 100 mobile; §5, faint streets, no pin);
  - an 8 px coral progress bar;
  - a small round ✕ quit button.
- **Bottom-left:** **3 item slots**, 64 px square, 18 px radius.
  - **Empty:** a dashed outline.
  - **Filled:** a white border, a 4 px lip and the item icon.
  - A tiny ink key hint sits on the corner: "Space", "2", "3".
  - "Bag full — use an item!" fades in above the slots in gel-red.
- **Bottom-centre** (bottom-right on mobile, stacked): the pace chip (zone dot + "Slow · real 5'22"") and a speed chip ("20.0 km/h").
- **Centre:** the countdown "3, 2, 1, GO!" in huge white Lexend with a coral offset shadow. It scales in from 1.6 and GO! fades out.
- **Banners:** "5K ENERGY GEL!", "BANANA GRIP!" and "SHINE MUSCAT SPRINT!" show at about 22 % from the top (smaller, with a red offset shadow) and fade after about 1 s.
- **Edge flashes** (radial vignettes): cyan for turbo, red for leaving the road, pink when an item is used.
- **Touch devices:** faint ◀ ▶ hints near the bottom corners.

### 2.7 Result and game over
- **Result:** a centred panel with these elements, top to bottom.
  - **Heading:** an eyebrow of "FINISH" or "NEW RECORD", then a title of "Finished!" or "New record!".
  - **Time:** in coral (64 px, tabular).
  - **Comparison line:** "x.xxs faster than past you!".
  - **Stat tiles (3×2):** Equiv. time, Equiv. pace, Coins (★ if it's a new best), Turbo · Items, Off road, Best.
  - **Buttons:** **Courses** (secondary) and **Run again** (primary).
- **Game over:** the eyebrow "GAME OVER", the title "Past you got away", one encouraging sentence with the % reached, and **Courses** / **Try again**.

### 2.8 Toast
A dark ink pill at the bottom centre with white 15 px text. It slides up 20 px and fades in, then disappears after about 2.6 s.

---

## 3. 3D art direction

### 3.1 World
- **Sky:** a vertical gradient dome from horizon `#ffe6cc` (peach) to zenith `#8ecbf0` (soft blue). There is **no sun disc**.
- **Fog:** linear, peach `#ffe6cc`, 70–320 units in the race. It retargets smoothly for wide overview shots.
- **Light:**
  - a hemisphere light, sky `#cfe9ff` over ground `#8dbf6a`, intensity 1.6;
  - a warm directional "late afternoon" sun `#fff1d6`, intensity 2.2, high and to one side, casting soft shadows near the player.
- **Shading:** **toon / cel shading with 3 soft steps.** Everything is matte. Nothing is metallic or glossy except tiny white glints on eyes and grapes.
- **Ground:** flat pastel grass `#9fd47a`, extending far past the course.
- **Grass blades:** dense tufts along both road shoulders (out to about 16 units). Each is a tapered 3-segment blade, dark `#5f9f45` at the base to light `#d8f0a0` at the tip, with gently varied hue. They **sway in a slow wind**: about 1.7 Hz plus a faster flutter, stronger at the tips, phase varying across the field.
- **Flowers:** small faceted blobs in white `#ffffff`, pink `#ffb3c7`, butter `#ffe17a` and lilac `#c9b6ff`, scattered near the road.
- **Lollipop trees:** a tapered tan trunk `#b98a62` with a big round faceted crown. Most crowns are greens (`#7cc26b`, `#94d070`, `#6db86a`); about 1 in 5 are blossom pink `#f6b8c8` or amber `#ffd38a`. Sizes vary from 0.8 to 1.5×. They stand 5–75 units from the road, never on it.
- **Bushes:** squashed round green blobs (`#6fb35a`, slight hue jitter) close to the shoulders.
- **Clouds:** 26 puffy clusters of 4–7 white balls, high in the sky. They are not affected by fog, so they stay crisp white.

### 3.2 Road
- **Surface:** 9 units wide, coloured by pace zone (the pace colours lifted 10 % toward cream `#fff8ec`). Colours blend smoothly where zones change.
- **Edges:** a sandy shoulder `#efdfc0`, 0.9 units wide, on each side, and a thin white edge line just inside each edge.
- **Overlapping passes** (out-and-back, U-turns): they read as **two side-by-side lanes with a strip of grass between**, joined by a rounded U. A crossing reads as a clean crossing. The stretch the runner is on is always drawn on top.
- **Start and finish:** a white stripe across the road at the start, and a dark `#333344` stripe at the finish. The finish has a **white arch**: two rounded pillars about 5.8 units tall, with a chunky banner across the top printed in a black-and-white checkerboard.
- **Turbo pads:** flat 3.2 × 4.5 rectangles in turbo-cyan with two white chevrons pointing forward, **scrolling forward** continuously. Used pads fade to 15 %.

### 3.3 Camera
- **Race:**
  - **Position:** behind and above the runner (about 8.5 back, 4.2 up), following the runner's smoothed heading so it swings calmly through hairpins. It looks about 10 units ahead at knee-to-hip height.
  - **Field of view:** 58°, widening slightly with speed, plus 10° during turbo and 4° while energized.
  - **Shake:** a small camera shake when leaving the road.
- **Intro:** from the overview shot, a smooth swoop down to the chase position (about 1.3 s), then the countdown.
- **Finish:** a slow orbit (about 6 units out) around the celebrating runner.
- **Menus:** a close, slow sway around the character's front; overview shots orbit high over the whole course.

---

## 4. Characters

All five are **soft toys built from simple rounded shapes**, matte, with gradients that run bottom → top across a part.

### 4.1 Shared rules
- **Proportions:** they stand about 1.6–2.1 units tall. Heads are big relative to bodies. Limbs are short and tube-like, with ball or bean shapes at the ends (hands, boots).
- **Rig:** one rig for all — body, head, two arms hanging from the shoulders, two legs from the hips.
- **Animation:**
  - **Run:** legs swing alternately and arms counter-swing. The stride gets faster with speed but caps out (toy legs). The body bobs, leans into steering and pitches forward, more during turbo.
  - **Idle:** a small bounce, with the head looking slowly left and right.
  - **Celebrate:** a bouncy jump with both arms straight up.
- **Ghost "Past You":** the same character, rendered as a flat, translucent pale blue `#bfe6ff` silhouette (about 40 % opacity) with no face details. It always runs in the lane to the runner's left. When it is **just behind the runner** (between the runner and the chase camera), it fades out completely within about 4 units, so it never blocks the view.
- **Faces:** facial features sit **on the front surface** of the head and are clearly visible, never sunk inside it.

### 4.2 The five runners
| Name · blurb | Look |
|---|---|
| **Hoodie** · Mint hood kid *(default main runner)* | A big round hood that runs pale butter-yellow `#ffe8a8` → `#ffe2a0`, with a thick rolled rim around a round peachy-pink face `#f6c1b4`. **Two teal bangs** `#7fd7d2` part in the middle of the forehead. **Googly eyes** (white with black pupils), pink cheeks and a small smile. A round body graded **mint `#5fcfcb` → pale mint → butter `#ffe6a4`** with two teal hoodie drawstrings. **Puffy sleeves** graded mint → butter, with tiny pink hands. Thin pink legs and **mint rain boots** with butter tops. Slightly smaller overall (0.85×). |
| **Sprout** · Leafy smiler | A big **bulb head** graded **teal-mint `#5fcfc4` → pale green `#cfe8b4` → butter `#fbecbc`**. On top: **two lilac/pink rings** (`#dca6ef`, `#f3c4f0`) and **three leaf sprouts** graded green `#a8d98a` → pale yellow. **Closed happy ∩ eyes**, a small orange nose `#f07d5e` and **plump coral lips** `#f2876a`. Body graded **purple `#c27be0` → pink → peach**, with pink shoulder pads. Mint arms and legs `#79dccb`, yellow cuffs and pink boots `#e7a6f2`. Scale 0.82. |
| **Dizzy** · Giggly sprout | A big **bulb head** graded **orange `#f19f68` → apricot → butter `#fbd98f`**. On top: **two teal rings** and **four pale-yellow leaves** fanning out. **✕✕ eyes** (two crossed dark bars each) and a **big open laughing mouth**: a red bowl `#e5435a` with a pink tongue `#ff8f9c` and a darker lip line, bulging slightly out of the face. A teal round body `#3fb5b0` with lighter teal shorts and round shoulder caps. **Long pink arms and legs** `#f4b8b2`, yellow cuffs `#f6c46a` and coral boots `#ee8b68`. Scale 0.82. |
| **Bloom** · Walking flower pot | The **head is the whole body**: an open-topped pot-sphere graded **pink `#e58fdc` → lilac `#cdb3e6` → mint `#9fd9d6` → mint `#7fdcc8`** from bottom to top. It has a mint rim, a dark-green soil disc and a **crown of seven mint leaves** leaning outward. From the pot rises a **curved stem with a six-petal pink flower** `#e78ae8` and a yellow centre `#f6d44a`. **Glossy black bead eyes** with a tiny white glint, and **plump yellow lips** `#f6d23f`. **Long, thin pink arms** `#f3b9df` that hang straight at the sides, and thin pink legs with pink→mint boots and mint cuffs. Scale 0.82. |
| **Beanie** · Cozy beanie walker | A round **teal head** `#3db4b8` wearing a **pale-yellow beanie**: a domed top `#f8dfa2` and a thick rolled brim `#fae5b0`. **Googly eyes**, pink cheeks and a smile. A **bell-shaped body** (wide at the hem, narrowing up to the neck) graded **coral `#f19478` → peach `#f6b07f` → butter `#fbd88e`**. Short peach sleeves, teal arms and hands, thin teal legs, butter cuffs and peach boots `#f39f74`. Scale 0.85. |

### 4.3 Thumbnails
Each character is shown on its card as a real render of the 3D model: a ¾ view from slightly above, the whole figure with headroom (leaves and flowers never cropped), on the sky→peach card gradient.

---

## 5. Route map style ("track on a street map")

Used on route cards, in the course preview and (more subtly) in the HUD minimap. North is up.

- **Streets:** a faint street network in the background, unique per route but stable.
  - Two near-perpendicular families of broken, slightly bent streets, plus short alleys.
  - Each street has a faint lilac edge, a soft blurred white body and a thin bright white core.
  - It fades out toward the edges like a vignette.
  - Strength: full on cards and in the preview, about 60 % in the HUD.
- **Track:** a soft coral glow (cards and preview only) under a white casing, with the line on top coloured by pace zone. It is 5 px on cards and preview, 3.5 px in the HUD.
- **Gels:** small red rounded rectangles with a white outline.
- **Start and finish:** round **coral dots** with a coral→deep-pink gradient, a white rim and a soft pink shadow. The **finish dot has a white centre**.
- **Pin:** a teardrop **map pin** at top-left (cards and preview), graded yellow `#ffc46b` → deep pink `#f2677d`, with a white hole.
- **Clearance:** the route never touches the corner tag or the pin; leave about 44 px of top padding on cards.
- **HUD extras:** a coral player dot and a pale-blue ghost dot, both with an ink outline.

---

## 6. Pickups and items

| Item | Look | Motion |
|---|---|---|
| **Coin** | An upright **soft matte toy coin** (radius 0.7, thick): orange edge band `#f39a3d`, slightly orange face `#fdbd4e`, and a thick pale-gold rounded rim `#ffd668` with a **raised "$"** on both sides. Tilted slightly forward. | Floats at about 1.1, bobs gently, spins. When collected it pops up and shrinks away in about 0.3 s. |
| **Energy gel** | A **soft pouch**: red body `#e8414f`, pink band and shoulder `#f4899a`, light pink tear-off cap `#f8a9b4`. A white label reads "ENERGY / **GEL** / ⚡" with the km mark, for example "5K". It is generic, with **no brand logo**. Scale about 1.6×. | Floats at about 1.8 with a gentle sway, over a pulsing pink ring on the ground. When picked up it rises and vanishes in about 0.4 s. |
| **Banana** | A curved yellow banana `#ffd84d` with a brown stem tip and a green end. | Floats at about 1.3, spins and bobs, over a pulsing butter ring. |
| **Shine muscat** | **One** glossy pale-green grape `#b8e86a` with a small white glint and a short stem. | Same as the banana, with a pale-green ring. |
| **Item slot icons** | Flat, friendly versions of the three: a red pouch with a lightning bolt, a yellow crescent banana, a green grape with a highlight. | The slot pops (1.35× → 1×) when an item lands in it. |

---

## 7. Motion and sound

### 7.1 Motion
- **Screen changes:** fade in over 0.35 s, and panels rise 24 px with a slight overshoot ("back-out") over 0.5 s.
- **Card hover:** lift and tilt in 0.18 s. **Button press:** 0.12 s.
- **Countdown digits:** scale 1.6 → 1 with a strong overshoot.
- **No motion is jarring:** cameras and colour changes always ease. Nothing flashes at full white.

### 7.2 Sound
All sounds are short synthesized tones, soft and cheerful, with no harsh noise:
- **Countdown:** ticks, then a brighter "GO".
- **Coin / item pickup:** a two-note chime.
- **Item use:** a rising arpeggio.
- **Turbo:** a short upward sweep.
- **Leaving the road:** a low bump.
- **Finish:** a rising four-note fanfare.
- **Game over:** three descending notes.

---

## 8. Design acceptance checklist

Capture each item at **1280×800** and **390×844** and compare it with this document.

1. The home screen shows the coral Hanalei Fill logo on two lines, one tagline, **My Runs** (coral) and **My Stats** (cream), and the main runner on the right (desktop) or on top (mobile).
2. The meadow is pastel with swaying grass, round lollipop trees (mostly green, some pink and amber), white puffy clouds, and a peach-to-blue sky with peach fog. It is never dark or saturated.
3. Route cards show the street-map art, coral glow, coral start/finish dots (white-centred finish), a pin at top-left and a km tag at top-right, with the route clear of both.
4. All five characters match §4.2, and none of their faces is hidden inside the head.
5. The road shows four distinct pace colours, with the mint clearly different from the grass. It has sandy shoulders and white edge lines, and the finish arch is checkered.
6. Out-and-back routes read as two lanes with a rounded U, and crossings are clean.
7. Coins are matte gold toy coins with a raised "$"; the gel pouch has no brand logo; the banana and the single grape are instantly recognisable.
8. HUD positions match §2.6, nothing overlaps on mobile, and the item slots are thumb-reachable.
9. Every title is Hanalei Fill without effects, and all other text is Lexend. All copy is English.

---

## 9. Inspiration (look and feel only)

These references set the direction. **No assets, code, sounds or text come from them.** Everything is made from scratch.

| Reference | What to take from it | What not to take |
|---|---|---|
| **Summer Afternoon** by Victor Lucendo, https://summer-afternoon.vlucendo.com/ | The mood: pastel colours, soft toon shading, wind-blown grass, round "lollipop" trees, warm golden-hour light, gentle fog, a calm and nostalgic feel | Its scene, models, characters, multiplayer or any asset |
| **Montblanc Legend Blue — "The Run"**, https://therun.montblanclegend.com/ | The game feel: auto-forward along a spline road, left/right steering only, turbo pads, slowing at the road edge with a camera shake, 3-2-1-GO countdown, time attack against a best time, cinematic finish | Any branding, logos, the car, product imagery, sounds or copy |
| **Fonts**: Hanalei Fill https://fonts.google.com/specimen/Hanalei+Fill and Lexend https://fonts.google.com/specimen/Lexend | Typography (§1.2) | — |

Character, coin and route-map designs were drawn after reference images that are **not** distributed (some carry stock watermarks). Use §4, §5 and §6 instead. Never put third-party stock images in the repository or the video.
```

### `docs/GEMINI_GUIDE.md`

12466 bytes · sha256 `31d0e3d88b95d366c251b00fdeffe856d84ddc53a1cbeef408179194d3c63367`

````markdown
# Driving the Build with Google Antigravity + Gemini 4 Argon

A step-by-step playbook for the team: what to give the agent, how to set it up, and the exact prompts to use for each phase of [`ARGON_BRIEF.md`](./ARGON_BRIEF.md), with the look defined by [`DESIGN.md`](./DESIGN.md). The goal is a build that is **written during the hackathon**, **verified at every step**, and **easy to show** in the demo.

---

## 0. Rules of engagement (read first)

| Do | Don't |
|---|---|
| Give the agent `ARGON_BRIEF.md`, `DESIGN.md`, and assets and data the team owns (your GPX runs, your app screenshots, your images) | Give it any pre-existing source code, `SPEC.md` or `REBUILD.md`. Demo code must be written during the event |
| Work **one phase at a time** and require Gate evidence before moving on | Ask for "the whole app" in one prompt |
| Make the agent **show proof**: test output, screenshots, trace logs | Accept "done" or "should work" without evidence |
| Commit after every passing step (small, descriptive commits) | Squash, rewrite history or backdate commits |
| Label anything cached or simulated, everywhere | Fake a verification loop or ship canned AI output as live |
| Keep model names and API calls exactly as the official docs say | Let the agent guess SDK shapes or model IDs |

---

## 1. One-time setup (≈15 min)

1. **Create the public GitHub repo** `pace-runner` with an MIT license and an empty README. Make the first commit at kickoff.
2. **Open it in Antigravity.** Choose **Gemini 4 Argon** (`gemini-4-argon`) as the agent model. Use the `antigravity-preview-09-2026` sandbox for running tests and browsers.
3. **Add the brief and the design:** copy `ARGON_BRIEF.md` into the repo as `docs/BRIEF.md`, and `DESIGN.md` as `docs/DESIGN.md`.
4. **Add team-owned fixtures:** put 3–5 of your own GPX runs and 2 running-app screenshots in `fixtures/`, and 3 images you own for the Character Forge in `fixtures/forge/`.
5. **Add standing rules** for the agent. Paste §2 into the workspace's agent rules / custom instructions, or into an `AGENTS.md` at the repo root if your setup reads one.
6. **Cloud access:** create the Google Cloud project, Firebase Hosting site and Cloud Run service account yourself, and give the agent only the project ID and region (`asia-northeast3`). Keep keys out of chat and out of the repo.

---

## 2. Standing rules (paste once)

```text
You are building "Pace Runner" for the hackathon, Track 1 (Entertainment).
The sources of truth are docs/BRIEF.md (what to build and how) and docs/DESIGN.md (how it must look).
Read both fully before any work and re-read the relevant sections at the start of every phase.
DESIGN.md is mandatory: do not invent your own colours, layouts, characters or fonts.

Working rules:
1. Work one phase at a time (BRIEF §5.4). Never start the next phase until the current Gate passes.
2. For every phase: write a short plan into docs/BUILD_LOG.md (timestamp, goal, files), implement in
   small steps, then VERIFY:
   - run the typecheck and the unit tests in the sandbox and paste the summary;
   - run the app in the browser, drive it with Playwright, and save screenshots at 1280x800 and 390x844;
   - compare the results with every line of the phase Gate, one by one, marked PASS/FAIL with evidence;
   - for anything visible, check your screenshots against the matching DESIGN.md section and the §8 checklist.
   If anything fails, fix it and verify again. Only then commit as "phase N: <what>".
3. Never claim something works without evidence you just produced. If you could not verify
   something, say exactly what and why.
4. Before writing any Gemini / Google Cloud integration, look up the official documentation for the
   exact model ID, SDK call, parameters and quotas. Never guess an API shape. Cite the doc page in BUILD_LOG.md.
5. The client never executes model-written code. Models return JSON that is validated against the
   schemas in packages/schemas before use.
6. Every AI feature has a verification loop and a non-AI fallback (BRIEF §3). Anything cached or
   simulated must be labelled "cached" / "simulated" in the UI and in the README.
7. All UI copy, code comments, docs and commit messages are in English.
8. Only use assets in fixtures/ or ones you generate. No copyrighted or watermarked images,
   no brand logos.
9. Keep the deterministic simulation in packages/sim free of DOM/WebGL so it runs in tests and on the server.
10. If a requirement in BRIEF.md is unclear or impossible, stop and ask instead of improvising.
```

---

## 3. Kickoff prompt

```text
Read docs/BRIEF.md and docs/DESIGN.md end to end. Then:
1. Summarise the product in 5 bullet points and list the 7 build phases with their Gates.
1b. Describe the visual style in 5 bullet points and the five characters in one line each, from
    DESIGN.md §4, so I can check you read the design correctly.
2. List every external API/model you will need and, for each, the official doc you will check.
3. List the questions or risks you see (missing info, quota, timing).
Do not write code yet.
```

Check the answer against the brief before going on. If the summary is off, correct it now: this is the cheapest moment to fix misunderstandings.

---

## 4. Phase prompts

Use these one at a time. Each ends with the same verification demand.

**Verification suffix:** add this to every phase prompt.

```text
When you think the phase is done: run typecheck + unit tests, run the app and capture screenshots at
1280x800 and 390x844, then give me a table with every Gate item from BRIEF §5.4 for this phase:
item | PASS/FAIL | evidence (test name, screenshot path, log line). Fix all FAILs before reporting.
Then append the evidence to docs/BUILD_LOG.md and commit.
```

### P0 — Setup
```text
Phase P0 (BRIEF §5.1–5.2, §5.4 row P0): create the monorepo layout (apps/web, apps/api,
packages/sim, packages/schemas, prompts, fixtures, docs), TypeScript strict, Vite client, Vitest,
Playwright, GitHub Actions CI (typecheck, tests, schema tests, build). Deploy a hello-world: Firebase
Hosting for apps/web, Cloud Run "pace-runner-api" with GET /api/health, and the /api/** rewrite.
```

### P1 — Core game (no AI yet)
```text
Phase P1 (BRIEF §2.3–2.4 and §5.4 row P1). Build the playable game without any model calls:
GPX import, course builder in packages/sim (pace bands, lanes for overlapping passes, U-turn rounding,
pads, coin rows, gels every 5 km, bananas and shine muscats), deterministic simulation with the ghost,
carried items (3 slots), controls, HUD, toon meadow, all screens incl. My Routes, My Stats and the main
runner, fonts. Write the unit tests listed in the P1 Gate first, then make them pass. Use the GPX files
in fixtures/ as real inputs, including the out-and-back + U-turn + figure-8 fixture.
```
Then check yourself: play one of your own runs on a phone. It must feel fair: a clean line beats the ghost narrowly.

### P2 — Level Director (A1) ★
```text
Phase P2 (BRIEF §3 A1, §4.1 LevelSpec, §5.4 row P2). Implement the Level Director agent: Argon gets
RunData + theme and returns a LevelSpec (JSON only, validated). Verification loop: schema/bounds check,
then headless playtests with the perfect/average/sloppy bots from packages/sim against the fairness
targets; on failure send Argon the measured numbers and iterate (max 4). Stream every iteration to the
Agent trace drawer. Add the "procedural (no AI)" fallback. Show me one full trace for each of 3 real runs.
```

### P3 — Character Forge (A2) ★
```text
Phase P3 (BRIEF §3 A2, §4.1 CharacterSpec, §5.4 row P3). First author the 5 built-in characters as
CharacterSpecs and render them, so the format is proven. Then build the Forge: nano-banana turnaround
sheet → Argon CharacterSpec → validate → headless renders (front, 3/4, side, run pose) → deterministic
checks (height, feet on ground, face parts in front of the head by raycast, symmetry, not cropped) →
Argon critique {score, pass, issues} → patch, max 5 iterations, pass at score ≥ 85 + all checks.
Approve/Discard UI. Prove the face-in-front check rejects a deliberately broken spec.
```

### P4 — Live rival + music (A3, A4)
```text
Phase P4 (BRIEF §3 A3–A4, §5.4 row P4). "Past You" on gemini-3.8-live with the tools listed in A3,
every call validated by the rules engine (ranges, placement legality, rate limit, tone filter) and
rejected calls returned with a reason; subtitles always on, mute toggle. Lyria stems from an Argon music
brief, mixed by pace zone / gap / turbo / gel. Record a race where ≥ 3 tool calls are accepted and one
illegal call is visibly rejected.
```

### P5 — Import + recap (A5, A6)
```text
Phase P5 (BRIEF §3 A5–A6, §5.4 row P5). Screenshot import with the numeric reconciliation loop and
the confirm overlay (Looks right / edit), tested on screenshots from 2 different apps in fixtures/.
Long-context recap over the whole history with stats tools; reject any number that isn't a tool result.
Show 5 recaps with 0 unsupported numbers.
```

### P6 — Deploy + demo data
```text
Phase P6 (BRIEF §5.4 row P6). Production deploy, per-IP rate limits, upload limits, daily budget guard
switching to clearly labelled cached mode. Seed the team's real runs. Verify on Chrome, Safari and a
real phone; verify the game still works with /api blocked.
```

### P7 — Submission
```text
Phase P7 (BRIEF §6). Write README.md (outline §6.2, incl. "What's live / cached / simulated" and
credits), the Project Description (§6.1, English) and a shot list for the 2-minute video (§6.3) with the
exact app states to prepare for each shot. Run the §6.4 checklist and report each item with evidence.
```

---

## 4b. Design check (after P1, and after any visual change)

```text
Design check against docs/DESIGN.md. For every screen in §2 (desktop 1280x800 and mobile 390x844), every
character in §4.2 and every pickup in §6, put the app in that state and capture a screenshot. Compare
each one with its DESIGN.md section and list every difference in colour (hex), layout, size, typography,
character shape or motion, citing the section. Fix the differences, recapture, and repeat until none are
left except intentional ones (list those with a reason). Then run the §8 checklist item by item.
```

Tip: the characters (DESIGN.md §4.2) are the most likely place for drift. Check eyes, mouths and colour gradients carefully.

## 5. Independent review pass (after P1, P3 and P6)

Open a **fresh agent session** so the reviewer isn't the author, and ask:

```text
You are reviewing, not building. Read docs/BRIEF.md and the current repository. Without changing code:
1. Run typecheck, tests and the app. 2. For the latest phase Gate, re-verify every item yourself.
3. List bugs, unverified claims, missing fallbacks, unlabelled cached/simulated content, any place a
model's output is used without validation, and any visual difference from docs/DESIGN.md. Rank by severity with file:line and a reproduction.
```

Send the findings back to the building session as a fix list.

---

## 6. When things go wrong

| Symptom | Prompt |
|---|---|
| The agent says "done" without proof | `Show the evidence table for every Gate item. If any item has no evidence, it is not done.` |
| It is looping on the same failure | `Stop. Write down the failing check, the 3 most likely causes and a test that distinguishes them. Run that test first.` |
| An API call fails or the shape is unclear | `Open the official documentation page for this model/SDK, quote the relevant signature, and fix the call to match it.` |
| Scope is creeping | `Re-read BRIEF §5.4. What is the next Gate? Do only what it requires.` |
| A visual looks wrong | `Take a screenshot, describe exactly what is wrong in it, fix it, and take the same screenshot again for comparison.` |
| Running out of time | Apply the cut order in BRIEF §5.4: A7 → A6 → synthesized music → GPX-only import. **Never cut A1, A2 or the trace drawer.** |

---

## 7. What to collect for the video and the description

As you go, save these to `docs/media/`:
- the Level Director trace with bot playtests passing;
- the Forge iteration strip with the score rising and the critique text;
- a rejected tool call from "Past You";
- the screenshot import reconciliation passing;
- a race on a real route with items and the ghost.

They are the evidence judges look for under "verification loop", and they make the 2-minute video easy to cut.
````

### `docs/SPEC.md`

34062 bytes · sha256 `a6838c5a1567bad31c9432096b546c9a16745394ba55e7ba96abb042e2b6d341`

````markdown
# Pace Runner — Project Specification

This document specifies **Pace Runner**, a web 3D running game: what it is, how it plays, the algorithms and constants behind it, its visuals, UI copy, tests and acceptance criteria.

---

## 1. Development setup and definition of done

1. **Toolchain**: Node.js **25.5.0**, pnpm **11.24.0** (`corepack enable && corepack prepare pnpm@11.24.0 --activate`). macOS or Linux.
2. **Dependencies**: pin the exact versions listed in §3 and commit the lockfile.
3. **Static checks**: `pnpm exec tsc --noEmit` prints nothing and exits 0.
4. **Tests**: `pnpm test` passes the suite described in §11 (**31 tests** across `src/route/route.test.ts`, `src/game/sim.test.ts` and `src/game/stats.test.ts`).
5. **Build**: `pnpm build` succeeds. A Vite "chunk larger than 500 kB" warning is expected (three.js) and is not an error.
6. **Behaviour**: `pnpm dev`, open the printed URL and walk through the acceptance checklist in §12.

---

## 2. Product summary

A web 3D running game. A real GPS run (from a phone or a GPX file) becomes the race course:

- the **route shape** comes from the GPS track,
- the **road colour** and the **per-segment top speed** come from the runner's real pace on that stretch,
- you race a **ghost ("past you")** that replays the same pacing; fall too far behind and it is game over.

The game mechanics follow the Montblanc "The Run" web game: auto-forward on a spline road, left/right steering only, turbo pads, slowdown off the road edge, a 3-2-1-GO countdown, time attack with a saved best time. The visual style follows *Summer Afternoon* (vlucendo): pastel colours, toon shading, wind-blown grass, round "lollipop" trees, soft fog. **No assets from either site are used**; everything is procedural.

All UI text is **English only**. There is no Korean anywhere in the app.

---

| Concern | Choice |
|---|---|
| Build / dev server | Vite 8.3.4 |
| Language | TypeScript 7.0.2, `strict`, `noUnusedLocals`, `noUnusedParameters`, `moduleResolution: bundler`, `noEmit` |
| 3D | three 0.186.1 (+ addons `BufferGeometryUtils.mergeGeometries`, `RoundedBoxGeometry`) |
| UI tweens | gsap 3.15.0 |
| Tests | vitest 5.0.4 (node environment, no DOM) |
| UI framework | **none** — vanilla DOM, one `index.html` with `<section>` screens |
| Backend / storage | none; `localStorage` only (best time, best coins, chosen character) |
| Fonts (Google Fonts) | **Hanalei Fill** for `h1`/`h2` titles (single weight, `font-synthesis: none`); **Lexend** 400–800 for everything else |
| Audio | WebAudio oscillators only (no audio files) |
````
